- META-001 [DECIDED] [IMPLEMENTED] Every nonempty line in this file contains one atomic rule or one unresolved decision point; evidence: this file.
- META-002 [DECIDED] [IMPLEMENTED] The `DECIDED` marker means the normative choice is frozen until this list is explicitly revised; evidence: this file.
- META-003 [DECIDED] [IMPLEMENTED] The `OPEN` marker means no normative choice has been made; evidence: this file.
- META-004 [DECIDED] [IMPLEMENTED] The `IMPLEMENTED` marker requires current repository evidence named in the same bullet; evidence: this file.
- META-005 [DECIDED] [IMPLEMENTED] The `NOT_IMPLEMENTED` marker means the normative rule is frozen but its implementation is incomplete; evidence: this file.
- META-006 [DECIDED] [IMPLEMENTED] The `BLOCKED` marker means implementation must not begin before the decision point is resolved; evidence: this file.
- META-007 [DECIDED] [IMPLEMENTED] The `AFTER` marker names prerequisites that must be resolved and evidenced before the current bullet may be marked `IMPLEMENTED`; evidence: this file.
- META-008 [DECIDED] [IMPLEMENTED] The `IN_PROGRESS` marker means at least one atomic child implementation slice is evidenced while the parent rule remains incomplete and grants no protocol-conformance claim until every required child is implemented and integrated; evidence: this file.
- META-009 [DECIDED] [IMPLEMENTED] A child rule has its parent rule's stable ID plus one zero-padded decimal segment, states one MR-sized implementation slice, and names current repository evidence when marked `IMPLEMENTED`; evidence: this file.
- META-010 [DECIDED] [IMPLEMENTED] When an `IN_PROGRESS` parent has a clean unblocked child slice, implementation work finishes that parent before starting an unrelated rule; evidence: this file.
- BASE-001 [DECIDED] [IMPLEMENTED] The deterministic mathematical checker alone decides whether a proof is mathematically valid; evidence: `crates/naome-checker/src/lib.rs`.
- BASE-002 [DECIDED] [IMPLEMENTED] Strict local artifact admission cannot accept a mathematically invalid proof; evidence: `crates/naome-ledger/src/lib.rs`.
- BASE-003 [DECIDED] [IMPLEMENTED] Current exact-head local selection admits artifacts only through strict canonical admission; evidence: `crates/naome-chain/src/block.rs`.
- BASE-004 [DECIDED] [IMPLEMENTED] An already selected `ProofId` is rejected as a duplicate; evidence: `crates/naome-checker/src/state.rs`.
- BASE-005 [DECIDED] [IMPLEMENTED] An already selected `DerivationId` is rejected as a duplicate; evidence: `crates/naome-checker/src/state.rs`.
- BASE-006 [DECIDED] [IMPLEMENTED] A new `DerivationId` proving an existing `StatementId` remains independently citable; evidence: `crates/naome-checker/src/state/tests.rs`.
- BASE-007 [DECIDED] [IMPLEMENTED] An already selected `DefinitionId` is rejected as a duplicate; evidence: `crates/naome-checker/src/state.rs`.
- BASE-008 [DECIDED] [IMPLEMENTED] Every `ArtifactBlock` commits exactly one `ArtifactId`; evidence: `crates/naome-chain/src/block.rs`.
- BASE-009 [DECIDED] [IMPLEMENTED] Current exact-head local selection resolves every artifact dependency from its selected parent state; evidence: `crates/naome-checker/src/state.rs`.
- BASE-010 [DECIDED] [IMPLEMENTED] Current exact-head artifact-block application changes selected state atomically or leaves it unchanged; evidence: `crates/naome-chain/src/block.rs`.
- BASE-011 [DECIDED] [IMPLEMENTED] An artifact-block identifier is an ancestry address rather than independent evidence of mathematical validity; evidence: `specs/proof-protocol.md`.
- BASE-012 [DECIDED] [IMPLEMENTED] Given one immutable caller-supplied position snapshot and one bounded caller-supplied signer-key list, the reference consensus kernel determines whether the listed active keys' exact weight is strictly greater than two thirds of the snapshot's unchanged total active weight; evidence: `crates/naome-consensus/src/lib.rs`, `crates/naome-consensus/src/tests.rs`.
- BASE-013 [DECIDED] [IMPLEMENTED] Given already-matured citation-reward atoms, the reference economy kernel creates exactly one initial Knowledge Weight unit per atom; evidence: `crates/naome-economy/src/lib.rs`, `crates/naome-economy/src/tests.rs`.
- BASE-014 [DECIDED] [IMPLEMENTED] Given one immutable Knowledge Weight origin batch and its caller-supplied integer age, the reference economy kernel evaluates exact overflow-safe linear decay to terminal zero at age 730; evidence: `crates/naome-economy/src/lib.rs`, `crates/naome-economy/src/tests.rs`; AFTER: `BASE-013`.
- BASE-015 [DECIDED] [IMPLEMENTED] Given caller-supplied already-computed artifact base-fee atoms F, the reference economy kernel assigns `floor(2 * F / 5)` citation atoms, `floor(F / 5)` validator atoms, and every remaining atom to burn with full conservation; evidence: `crates/naome-economy/src/lib.rs`, `crates/naome-economy/src/tests.rs`.
- BASE-016 [DECIDED] [IMPLEMENTED] Given caller-supplied already-computed non-artifact operation-fee atoms F, the reference economy kernel assigns zero citation atoms, `floor(F / 5)` validator atoms, and every remaining atom to burn with full conservation; evidence: `crates/naome-economy/src/lib.rs`, `crates/naome-economy/src/tests.rs`.
- BASE-017 [DECIDED] [IMPLEMENTED] Given one caller-supplied `FeePartition` value's citation pool P and one caller-validated distinct eligible-target count N, the reference economy kernel returns an arithmetic summary with per-target reward zero and burned remainder P when N is zero, otherwise per-target reward `floor(P / N)` and burned remainder `P mod N`, with exact conservation and no target-identity, credit, or state authority; evidence: `crates/naome-economy/src/lib.rs`, `crates/naome-economy/src/tests.rs`; AFTER: `BASE-015`.

- SEC-001 [DECIDED] [NOT_IMPLEMENTED] Block production is restricted to the canonical active validator set while validator candidacy remains permissionless.
- SEC-002 [DECIDED] [NOT_IMPLEMENTED] Any account that pays the required charges may submit a canonically valid artifact.
- SEC-003 [DECIDED] [NOT_IMPLEMENTED] Anyone may run a full independently verifying node.
- SEC-004 [DECIDED] [NOT_IMPLEMENTED] No trusted sequencer is permitted.
- SEC-005 [DECIDED] [NOT_IMPLEMENTED] An explicitly user-selected recent finalized checkpoint may bootstrap synchronization but grants no consensus authority and is never selected silently by peers or project infrastructure.
- SEC-006 [DECIDED] [NOT_IMPLEMENTED] The selected Sybil-resistance family is Proof of Cited Knowledge.
- SEC-007 [DECIDED] [NOT_IMPLEMENTED] No two honest conforming nodes finalize different consensus blocks at one height while Byzantine active agreement weight is strictly less than one third.
- SEC-008 [DECIDED] [NOT_IMPLEMENTED] Honest conforming nodes eventually finalize valid available proposals after eventual synchrony when strictly more than two thirds of active agreement weight follows the protocol and remains available.
- SEC-009 [DECIDED] [NOT_IMPLEMENTED] The safety proof assumes Byzantine active agreement weight is strictly less than one third of the immutable position snapshot.
- SEC-010 [DECIDED] [NOT_IMPLEMENTED] Consensus safety does not assume bounded message delay.
- SEC-011 [DECIDED] [NOT_IMPLEMENTED] Consensus liveness assumes eventual synchrony and increasing round timeouts.
- SEC-012 [DECIDED] [NOT_IMPLEMENTED] A partition without strict greater-than-two-thirds available agreement weight halts finality rather than relaxing quorum or producing conflicting finalized history.
- SEC-013 [DECIDED] [NOT_IMPLEMENTED] After synchrony and strict greater-than-two-thirds availability return, validators resume round progression without rewriting finalized history.
- SEC-014 [DECIDED] [NOT_IMPLEMENTED] Creating or selecting a valid artifact alone grants no consensus power.
- SEC-015 [DECIDED] [NOT_IMPLEMENTED] `ArtifactId` count contributes zero consensus security weight.
- SEC-016 [DECIDED] [NOT_IMPLEMENTED] `ProofId` count contributes zero consensus security weight.
- SEC-017 [DECIDED] [NOT_IMPLEMENTED] `DerivationId` count contributes zero consensus security weight.
- SEC-018 [DECIDED] [NOT_IMPLEMENTED] `StatementId` count contributes zero consensus security weight.
- SEC-019 [DECIDED] [NOT_IMPLEMENTED] Claimed mathematical importance contributes zero consensus security weight.
- SEC-020 [DECIDED] [NOT_IMPLEMENTED] Claimed mathematical novelty contributes zero consensus security weight.
- SEC-021 [DECIDED] [NOT_IMPLEMENTED] Consensus-critical numeric calculations use exact deterministic arithmetic.
- SEC-022 [DECIDED] [NOT_IMPLEMENTED] Consensus-critical numeric calculations forbid floating-point arithmetic.
- SEC-023 [DECIDED] [NOT_IMPLEMENTED] Consensus safety does not depend on local execution speed.
- SEC-024 [DECIDED] [NOT_IMPLEMENTED] A timeout cannot make an invalid block valid.
- SEC-025 [DECIDED] [NOT_IMPLEMENTED] Weighted Tendermint consensus uses signed proposal, prevote, and precommit control messages distinct from consensus blocks; AFTER: `SEC-006`.
- SEC-026 [DECIDED] [NOT_IMPLEMENTED] An artifact that remains admissible, fully available, and highest-ranked when an honest proposer begins proposal construction is included in that round's proposal.
- SEC-027 [DECIDED] [NOT_IMPLEMENTED] Empty consensus progress does not satisfy liveness while an admissible artifact awaits inclusion.
- SEC-028 [DECIDED] [NOT_IMPLEMENTED] Temporary genesis-validator bootstrap weight is the only permitted consensus weight not backed by matured protocol citation rewards.
- SEC-029 [DECIDED] [NOT_IMPLEMENTED] Competitive hash mining contributes zero consensus weight.
- SEC-030 [DECIDED] [NOT_IMPLEMENTED] Partitioning one fixed amount of economically backed Knowledge Weight across identities creates no additional Knowledge Weight and cannot make one live Knowledge Weight unit contribute more than once.
- SEC-031 [DECIDED] [NOT_IMPLEMENTED] Post-bootstrap consensus power derives only from matured Knowledge Weight backed by protocol citation rewards.
- SEC-032 [DECIDED] [NOT_IMPLEMENTED] The safety model counts adversary-controlled effective agreement weight in each immutable active-set snapshot regardless of how that weight was acquired or partitioned across validator keys.
- SEC-033 [DECIDED] [NOT_IMPLEMENTED] An honest proposer with no lock-required valid value selects the fully validated available artifact candidate with the highest inclusion bid; AFTER: `SEC-008`, `SEC-011`, `SEC-026`.
- SEC-040 [DECIDED] [NOT_IMPLEMENTED] Equal highest inclusion bids are ordered by ascending `ArtifactId`; AFTER: `SEC-033`.
- SEC-041 [DECIDED] [NOT_IMPLEMENTED] V1 has no forced-inclusion queue, so its inclusion guarantee is conditional on the candidate remaining highest-ranked when an honest proposer begins construction; AFTER: `SEC-026`, `SEC-033`.

- SAFE-002 [DECIDED] [NOT_IMPLEMENTED] If the selected finality model exposes irreversible checkpoints, no two honest conforming nodes accept conflicting irreversible checkpoints under the selected adversary assumptions.
- SAFE-003 [DECIDED] [NOT_IMPLEMENTED] If the selected finality model exposes irreversible checkpoints, every irreversible checkpoint has passed strict canonical artifact validation.
- SAFE-004 [DECIDED] [NOT_IMPLEMENTED] If the selected finality model exposes irreversible checkpoints, every irreversible checkpoint has satisfied the selected data-availability rule.
- SAFE-005 [DECIDED] [NOT_IMPLEMENTED] If the selected finality model exposes irreversible checkpoints, every irreversible checkpoint descends from the prior irreversible checkpoint.

- SAFE-007 [DECIDED] [NOT_IMPLEMENTED] Honest conforming nodes eventually make progress under the selected liveness assumptions.
- SAFE-008 [DECIDED] [NOT_IMPLEMENTED] Fork choice considers only fully validated candidates.
- SAFE-009 [DECIDED] [NOT_IMPLEMENTED] Fork choice considers only candidates satisfying the selected data-availability rule.

Candidate-backed vectors separately accept current, lower, and higher evidence rounds bounded by both established ceilings; routing and ceiling precedence, missing candidate then payload insertion and retry, duplicate-signer and insufficient-weight batches, proposal-authorization and vote-position mismatch, and pending-signer post-finality failure prove the pre-effect return-scope versus post-finality consume-and-restart boundary and preserve candidate diagnostic metadata. - TEST-297-001 [DECIDED] [IMPLEMENTED] Unix fixed-validator node integration tests prove exact caller-owned higher-round proposal deferral and later full re-verification: a valid routed `P > R` proposal from each healthy Proposal, Prevote, or Precommit phase returns a token containing byte-identical control and payload plus exact verified descriptors while preserving live position, phase, populated lock and valid value where present, and all four authority images; after the source round and signing callback are dropped and the node independently progresses to `P/Proposal`, consuming the token into raw inputs and submitting them through existing current-round voting produces the exact anchored prevote and strict restart restores `P/Prevote`. Post-deferral control and payload tampering, premature and stale current-round vote use, non-higher and above-ceiling routes, malformed proposal control, wrong authenticated round, mismatched payload, and invalid optional proof reject without an effect. A pre-existing pending higher-round checkpoint is instead detected first, consumes the scope, causes no additional authority write, and strict reopen recovers the checkpoint. Together these vectors prove the token is neither cached validity nor progression authority; evidence: `crates/naome-node/src/fixed_validator_startup/tests/proposal_deferral.rs`; AFTER: `NET-020-001`, `PROD-080-001`, `PROD-020-024`, `TEST-409`, `TEST-410`.

They retain independently valid byte-distinct evidence variants for one proposal root and a competing root, require both exact inputs for retrieval, and cover separate item and byte saturation with lossless attempted-token return, unchanged retained state, later duplicate and distinct insertion denial, retrieval denial, and lossless drain-and-reset reuse. The same separately owned buffer survives its signing-callback return and a same-process strict signer reopen with exact live and four-file authority observations; after independent progression, three exactly retrieved variants with producer-signature, optional-proof-signature, or payload tampering reject without authority mutation, while an untampered variant succeeds only through the existing fully verifying, durably anchored vote consumer and strict reopen restores the resulting round and phase. Fresh construction proves only absence of a reconstruction path, not process-restart runtime behavior, and collection-reservation failure remains unforced; evidence: `crates/naome-node/src/fixed_validator_startup/proposal_buffer.rs`, `crates/naome-node/src/fixed_validator_startup/tests/proposal_buffer.rs`; AFTER: `PROD-080-002`, `TEST-297-001`.
- TEST-297-003 [DECIDED] [IMPLEMENTED] Unix fixed-validator node integration exercises the exact buffered-proposal/prebuilt-certificate composition: a byte-exact round-two token plus its fully verified same-position `Prevote/Proposal(root)` quorum reaches only the matching anchored precommit, returns the exact removed token, preserves both a byte-distinct same-root evidence variant and a competing-root sibling plus both finality files, and strict reopen restores `P/Precommit` with the exact lock and complete valid-value proof. Sequential missing-address, wrong-round, precommit-role, nil-target, competing-root, malformed-certificate, and caller-ceiling cases preserve the token, signer state, and all four authority images before a valid retry; deny-only saturation likewise rejects without effect until lossless drain-and-reset. Deterministic signer-anchor failures at the higher-round checkpoint, precommit preparation, and precommit completion each return no scope or vote, restore the exact token, preserve finality, and make strict reopen fail `AnchorBehind`; evidence: `crates/naome-node/src/fixed_validator_startup/tests/proposal_buffer.rs`; AFTER: `PROD-020-025`, `PROD-080-003`, `TEST-297-002`, `TEST-409`, `TEST-410`.
- TEST-297-004 [DECIDED] [IMPLEMENTED] Unix fixed-validator node integration exercises the exact buffered-proposal/signed-prevote-batch composition without claiming finality: a matching batch produces the same completed signed precommit, signer journal and anchor, returned exact token, retained same-root and competing-root siblings, unchanged finality files, and strict `P/Precommit` restart state as the prebuilt-certificate path. Empty-batch input proves caller round-capacity, saturation, and exact-address rejection precede batch parsing, then reaches the exact empty-batch rejection for a fully admitted token; the full all-or-nothing construction matrix rejects over-bound, malformed, foreign-context, wrong-position, wrong-role, wrong-target, duplicate, inactive, invalid-signature, and insufficient-weight batches while restoring the exact token, preserving the unchanged scope and all four authority images, and permitting a valid retry. A checkpoint-anchor failure returns no scope or vote, restores the token, preserves finality, and has the same strict `AnchorBehind` restart classification as the certificate path; evidence: `crates/naome-node/src/fixed_validator_startup/tests/proposal_buffer.rs`; AFTER: `PROD-020-026`, `PROD-080-004`, `TEST-093-001`, `TEST-297-003`, `TEST-409`, `TEST-410`.

Typed-round admission rejects wrong position, role, nil target, and inactive signers before retention. Two independently valid same-semantic-vote signature variants and two independently valid same-root proposal-evidence variants inserted in opposite orders select byte-identical lexicographic representatives, produce the same canonical certificate and anchored precommit, remove only the selected proposal, preserve every vote and the other proposal variant, leave both finality files unchanged, and strictly restart at the exact locked `P/Precommit` state.
